+ALF-K15 (2026-10-22) adds one more first-party module that a kernel file imports:
+`plugins/pipeline-core/lib/onboarding-driver-output.mjs` (the wire shape of the
+onboarding driver result that `scripts/onboarding-init.mjs` prints: each published
+ask and the primary collect-input action are printed once, with a resolver for
+consumers; it imports nothing and executes with kernel authority).
+
 This source closure does not establish protected-source delivery, independent
 review, native qualification or activation.
 
+ALF-K32 adds one further first-party module that a kernel file imports and that
+executes with kernel authority:
+`plugins/pipeline-core/scripts/advisory-admitted-consult.mjs` (the admitted half of
+the ordinary Codex consult; `scripts/advisory-host-bridge.mjs` hands it the result of a
+sealed host-owned executor, and it reports an answer only when
+`lib/advisory-answered-receipt.mjs` binds that result). It has no I/O of its own, imports
+only kernel paths and `lib/advisory-lifecycle-v2.mjs` and `lib/advisory-receipt-assurance.mjs`
+(both already kernel paths), and is itself one.
+

 }
+
+/**
+ * Backlog pipeline.git-stash-list-classified-as-working-tree-write (PF-16): which operands of a `sed` invocation are
+ * FILES. GNU sed takes the first non-option operand as the SCRIPT unless a script is given by `-e`/`-f`/`--expression`/
+ * `--file`, in which case every non-option operand is a file; the old whole-argv walk reported the script (and the value
+ * of `-e`/`-f`) as the written file, so a denial named `s/^...` instead of the real target. Options with a value are
+ * consumed (`-e S`, `-eS`, `--expression=S`, `-f F`, `-l N`, `--line-length=N`, an unambiguous long-option prefix); `-i[SUFFIX]`
+ * and `--in-place[=SUFFIX]` take a GLUED suffix only and end their short cluster (`-ni`, `-i.bak`, `-ie` = suffix `e`).
+ * A bare `-i` followed by an empty argument is the BSD detached empty suffix and the GNU empty script at once, so both
+ * readings stay candidates. An unrecognised option is a boolean. With `-i`, the filenames of `w`/`W` commands and of an
+ * `s///w` flag in a script are written too and are returned as targets. `inPlace` is the parsed fact; callers OR it with
+ * their own looser detection, so nothing that was refused before stops being refused.
+ */
+const SED_LONG_OPTIONS = new Map([
+  ["expression", "script"], ["file", "script-file"], ["line-length", "value"], ["in-place", "in-place"],
+  ["binary", null], ["debug", null], ["follow-symlinks", null], ["null-data", null], ["zero-terminated", null],
+  ["posix", null], ["quiet", null], ["silent", null], ["regexp-extended", null], ["sandbox", null],
+  ["separate", null], ["unbuffered", null], ["version", null], ["help", null],
+]);
+
+function sedScriptWriteFiles(script) {
+  const files = [];
+  const command = /(?:^|[;{}\n])[\s\d,$~+!]*(?:\/(?:\\.|[^/\\])*\/[\s!]*)?[wW][ \t]*([^\s;}]+)/gu;
+  const flag = /(?:^|[\s;{}!])s(.)(?:\\.|(?!\1).)*?\1(?:\\.|(?!\1).)*?\1[gpiImMeE0-9]*w[ \t]*([^\s;}]+)/gu;
+  for (const match of script.matchAll(command)) files.push(match[1]);
+  for (const match of script.matchAll(flag)) files.push(match[2]);
+  return files;
+}
+
+export function sedWriteTargets(argv) {
+  let inPlace = false, scriptByOption = false, ambiguousSuffix = false, afterDashDash = false;
+  const operands = [], scripts = [];
+  for (let index = 0; index < argv.length; index += 1) {
+    const arg = argv[index];
+    if (afterDashDash || arg === "-" || !arg.startsWith("-")) { operands.push(arg); continue; }
+    if (arg === "--") { afterDashDash = true; continue; }
+    if (arg.startsWith("--")) {
+      const equals = arg.indexOf("=");
+      const given = arg.slice(2, equals === -1 ? undefined : equals);
+      const name = SED_LONG_OPTIONS.has(given) ? given : [...SED_LONG_OPTIONS.keys()].filter((key) => key.startsWith(given)).length === 1
+        ? [...SED_LONG_OPTIONS.keys()].find((key) => key.startsWith(given)) : null;
+      const kind = name === null ? null : SED_LONG_OPTIONS.get(name);
+      if (kind === "in-place") inPlace = true;
+      if (kind === "script" || kind === "script-file" || kind === "value") {
+        const value = equals === -1 ? argv[index += 1] : arg.slice(equals + 1);
+        if (kind === "script") { scriptByOption = true; scripts.push(value ?? ""); }
+        if (kind === "script-file") scriptByOption = true;
+      }
+      continue;
+    }
+    for (let at = 1; at < arg.length; at += 1) {
+      const flag = arg[at], rest = arg.slice(at + 1);
+      if (flag === "i") {
+        inPlace = true;
+        if (rest === "" && argv[index + 1] === "") { ambiguousSuffix = true; index += 1; }
+        break;
+      }
+      if (flag === "e" || flag === "f" || flag === "l") {
+        const value = rest !== "" ? rest : argv[index += 1];
+        if (flag !== "l") scriptByOption = true;
+        if (flag === "e") scripts.push(value ?? "");
+        break;
+      }
+    }
+  }
+  if (!scriptByOption && operands.length > 0) scripts.push(operands[0]);
+  const files = scriptByOption || ambiguousSuffix ? operands : operands.slice(1);
+  return { inPlace, targets: inPlace ? [...files, ...scripts.flatMap(sedScriptWriteFiles)] : files };
+}

+// SPDX-License-Identifier: SUL-1.0
+/**
+ * PO decision D117 (decision BI of backlog item denial-names-a-diagnosis-it-refuses, second half): an approved
+ * feature whose approval cannot be RE-VERIFIED degrades to ONE advisory instead of blocking; a genuinely invalid
+ * approval keeps blocking with its current code. "Cannot be re-verified" is deliberately narrow: a verification INPUT
+ * is unavailable (today: the approved design workflow package file is missing, DWP-PACKAGE-PHYSICAL), never a mismatch.
+ *
+ * Both lanes are exercised against the same project: the Edit|Write hook as a process and the Bash lane
+ * (GUARD-DEVPLAN-SHELL) through evaluateLifecycleReadyGuard. The advisory travels on the channel the guard already
+ * uses for admission notices: exit 0 with the text on stderr.
+ *
+ * RED on the unchanged guard: the missing-package cases are refused (exit 2, DWP-PACKAGE-PHYSICAL) there. The negative
+ * controls are green before and after, which is what shows the degradation did not widen into a bypass.
+ */
+import assert from "node:assert/strict";
+import { execFileSync, spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
+import { createHash } from "node:crypto";
+import { mkdirSync, mkdtempSync, rmSync, writeFileSync } from "node:fs";
+import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
+import { dirname, join } from "node:path";
+import { after, test } from "node:test";
+import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
+
+import { createGovernanceScopeController } from "../lib/governance-scope.mjs";
+import { sha256CanonicalJson } from "../lib/plan-spec-state-v2.mjs";
+import {
+  UNVERIFIABLE_APPROVAL_CODES, UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE, designAdvisoryAdmission, unverifiedApprovalAdvisory,
+} from "../lib/guard-devplan-policy.mjs";
+import { evaluateLifecycleReadyGuard } from "./guard-lifecycle-ready.mjs";
+
+const GUARD = fileURLToPath(new URL("./guard-devplan.mjs", import.meta.url));
+const FEATURE_ID = "unverified-feature";
+const PLAN_PATH = "specs/feature/prd.md";
+const SPEC_PATH = "specs/feature/spec.md";
+const PACKAGE_PATH = "evidence/package.json";
+const PLAN_BYTES = "# PRD\n";
+const SPEC_BYTES = "# Spec\n";
+const PACKAGE_BYTES = '{"schema":"fixture"}\n';
+const MANIFEST_BLOCKING = "schema: pipeline.manifest.v0\ngates:\n  dev-plan:\n    mode: blocking\n    type: human\n";
+const sha256 = (value) => createHash("sha256").update(value).digest("hex");
+const DIRS = [];
+after(() => { for (const dir of DIRS) rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true }); });
+
+function writeText(dir, rel, text) {
+  const target = join(dir, ...rel.split("/"));
+  mkdirSync(dirname(target), { recursive: true });
+  writeFileSync(target, text);
+}
+
+/** A governed git repo whose state carries a v7 approval naming PACKAGE_PATH. `variant` shapes the one thing under test. */
+function fixture({ packageBytes = null, recordedPackageSha = sha256(PACKAGE_BYTES), planApproved = true, withApproval = true } = {}) {
+  const dir = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "guard-devplan-unverified-"));
+  DIRS.push(dir);
+  const git = (...args) => execFileSync("git", ["-C", dir, ...args], { encoding: "utf8", timeout: 20_000 }).trim();
+  git("init", "--quiet");
+  git("config", "user.email", "fixture@example.invalid");
+  git("config", "user.name", "Fixture");
+  const governance = createGovernanceScopeController({ hostStateRoot: join(dir, "fixture-host-state") });
+  const plan = governance.planDecision({ rootDir: dir, decision: "enroll", by: "disposable-guard-fixture" });
+  const active = governance.applyDecision(plan, { activate: true, planSha256: plan.planSha256 });
+  assert.equal(active.state, "active", "fixture enrollment must become active");
+  writeText(dir, PLAN_PATH, PLAN_BYTES);
+  writeText(dir, SPEC_PATH, SPEC_BYTES);
+  if (packageBytes !== null) writeText(dir, PACKAGE_PATH, packageBytes);
+  const submission = {
+    schema: "pipeline.plan-submission.v1", featureId: FEATURE_ID, planPath: PLAN_PATH, planSha256: sha256(PLAN_BYTES),
+    specPath: SPEC_PATH, specSha256: sha256(SPEC_BYTES), profile: "feature", profileSha256: "3".repeat(64),
+    submittedBy: "Coordinator", submittedAt: "2026-10-22T09:00:00.000Z",
+  };
+  const approval = {
+    schema: "pipeline.plan-approval.v7", approvedBy: "PO", approvedAt: "2026-10-22T10:00:00.000Z",
+    submissionSha256: sha256CanonicalJson(submission), profileSha256: submission.profileSha256,
+    poGateAuthority: {
+      schema: "pipeline.po-gate-authority.v2", humanFacing: "en", sourceSha256: "1".repeat(64), runtimeSha256: "2".repeat(64),
+      receiptSha256: "3".repeat(64), repositoryFingerprint: "7".repeat(64), planPath: PLAN_PATH, planSha256: submission.planSha256,
+      specPath: SPEC_PATH, specSha256: submission.specSha256,
+    },
+    priorInvalidationSha256: null, designAdvisorAdmissionSha256: null,
+    designWorkflowPackagePath: PACKAGE_PATH, designWorkflowPackageSha256: recordedPackageSha,
+    designWorkflowApproval: {
+      schema: "pipeline.design-workflow-package-approval.v1", mode: "chat", approvedBy: "PO", approvedAt: "2026-10-22T10:00:00.000Z",
+      packageSha256: recordedPackageSha, intentSha256: null, proofSha256: null, proof: null,
+    },
+  };
+  const state = {
+    schema: "pipeline.state.v0",
+    activeFeature: { id: FEATURE_ID, planPath: PLAN_PATH, phase: "implementation" },
+    planApproved,
+    planSubmission: submission,
+    ...(withApproval ? { planApproval: approval } : {}),
+  };
+  writeText(dir, ".claude/pipeline.yaml", MANIFEST_BLOCKING);
+  writeText(dir, ".claude/pipeline-state.json", JSON.stringify(state));
+  return { dir, state, git };
+}
+
+function editLane(dir) {
+  const result = spawnSync(process.execPath, [GUARD], {
+    input: JSON.stringify({ tool_name: "Edit", tool_input: { file_path: "src/foo.ts", old_string: "a", new_string: "b" } }),
+    encoding: "utf8", env: { ...process.env, CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR: dir }, timeout: 60_000,
+  });
+  return { code: result.status, stderr: String(result.stderr ?? "") };
+}
+function shellLane(dir) {
+  const result = evaluateLifecycleReadyGuard(
+    { tool_name: "Bash", tool_input: { command: "cp scratch/change.ts src/foo.ts" } },
+    { projectDir: dir, requireProjectOnboardingReadyFn: () => ({ schema: "pipeline.project-onboarding-ready-gate.v1", status: "ready", intent: "session" }) },
+  );
+  return { code: result.exitCode, stderr: String(result.stderr ?? "") };
+}
+const occurrences = (text, needle) => text.split(needle).length - 1;
+const LANES = [["Edit|Write hook", editLane], ["Bash lane (GUARD-DEVPLAN-SHELL)", shellLane]];
+
+test("precondition: the missing-package fixture reaches admission and designAdvisoryAdmission still reports it as NOT authority", () => {
+  const { dir, state } = fixture();
+  const admission = designAdvisoryAdmission(state, dir, PLAN_PATH, SPEC_PATH);
+  assert.equal(admission.ok, false);
+  assert.equal(admission.code, "DWP-PACKAGE-PHYSICAL");
+});
+
+for (const [label, lane] of LANES) {
+  test(`D117 ${label}: approval record present, package file missing -> allowed with ONE self-describing advisory`, () => {
+    const { dir } = fixture();
+    const run = lane(dir);
+    assert.equal(run.code, 0, `must be allowed, got ${run.code}: ${run.stderr.slice(0, 400)}`);
+    assert.equal(occurrences(run.stderr, UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE), 1, `exactly one typed advisory: ${run.stderr}`);
+    assert.ok(run.stderr.includes("DWP-PACKAGE-PHYSICAL"), "names the cause");
+    assert.ok(run.stderr.includes(PACKAGE_PATH), "names what could not be verified");
+    assert.match(run.stderr, /Nothing was verified/u);
+    assert.equal(occurrences(run.stderr, "reopen-design"), 1, "names the single repair command");
+    assert.ok(!run.stderr.includes("BLOCKED"), "an advisory is not a block");
+  });
+
+  test(`negative control ${label}: package present but its digest differs from the approval (tampered) -> still blocked with its own code`, () => {
+    const { dir } = fixture({ packageBytes: '{"schema":"tampered"}\n' });
+    const run = lane(dir);
+    assert.equal(run.code, 2, `must stay blocked: ${run.stderr.slice(0, 400)}`);
+    assert.ok(run.stderr.includes("DWP-APPROVAL-DIGEST-DRIFT"), run.stderr);
+    assert.ok(!run.stderr.includes(UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE), "no advisory on a mismatch");
+  });
+
+  test(`negative control ${label}: approval revoked (planApproved false) with the package missing -> still blocked, no advisory`, () => {
+    const { dir } = fixture({ planApproved: false });
+    const run = lane(dir);
+    assert.equal(run.code, 2, `must stay blocked: ${run.stderr.slice(0, 400)}`);
+    assert.ok(!run.stderr.includes(UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE), "a revoked approval never degrades");
+  });
+
+  test(`negative control ${label}: no approval record at all -> still blocked, no advisory`, () => {
+    const { dir } = fixture({ withApproval: false });
+    const run = lane(dir);
+    assert.equal(run.code, 2, `must stay blocked: ${run.stderr.slice(0, 400)}`);
+    assert.ok(!run.stderr.includes(UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE));
+  });
+}
+
+test("negative control: only an unavailable verification INPUT degrades; every mismatch, wrong-signer, wrong-candidate and setup code does not", () => {
+  assert.deepEqual([...UNVERIFIABLE_APPROVAL_CODES], ["DWP-PACKAGE-PHYSICAL"]);
+  const state = { planApproval: { designWorkflowPackagePath: PACKAGE_PATH } };
+  assert.equal(unverifiedApprovalAdvisory({ ok: false, code: "DWP-PACKAGE-PHYSICAL" }, state)?.code, UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE);
+  for (const code of [
+    "DWP-APPROVAL-DIGEST-DRIFT", "DWP-APPROVAL-SOURCE-DRIFT", "DWP-APPROVAL-PLAN-SPEC-MISMATCH", "DWP-APPROVAL-FEATURE-MISMATCH",
+    "DWP-APPROVAL-RECORD-DRIFT", "DWP-APPROVAL-INTENT-DRIFT", "DWP-BOUND-PATH-MODIFIED", "DWP-PACKAGE-SHAPE",
+    "DAA-PO-APPROVAL-TRUST-MISMATCH", "DAA-PO-APPROVAL-INVALID", "DAA-PO-APPROVAL-TRUST-ANCHOR-SETUP", "DAA-PO-APPROVAL-TRUST-POLICY",
+    "DAA-PO-APPROVAL-RECORD-DRIFT", "DAA-DESIGN-APPROVAL-BINDING-DRIFT", "DAA-DESIGN-SOURCE-DRIFT", "DAA-PUBLIC-UNAVAILABLE",
+    "DAA-UNAVAILABLE-FINAL-APPROVAL", "DAA-PRIVATE-INVALID",
+  ]) assert.equal(unverifiedApprovalAdvisory({ ok: false, code }, state), null, `${code} must keep blocking`);
+  assert.equal(unverifiedApprovalAdvisory({ ok: true }, state), null);
+  assert.equal(unverifiedApprovalAdvisory(undefined, state), null);
+});

+// ALF-K35 (PO decision 2026-10-22): the calls `extractWorkflowDispatches` skips by design are not silent for a Goldfish.
+// Scans the same `agentType` anchors with the same window and reports every call whose role is Goldfish (the predicate
+// `requiresGovernedItemAdmission` uses) and whose prompt cannot be resolved statically: a `${` interpolation, a value that
+// is not a string literal (an identifier or expression), or a literal that does not terminate. Advisory only: the caller
+// admits the call and records nothing for it. Critic and other roles are out of scope of V-10.
+export function findUnresolvedWorkflowGoldfishPrompts(script) {
+  const unresolved = [];
+  const agentTypeRe = /agentType\s*:\s*(['"])((?:(?!\1)[\s\S])*?)\1/g;
+  let m;
+  while ((m = agentTypeRe.exec(script)) !== null) {
+    const agentType = m[2];
+    if (!requiresGovernedItemAdmission(dispatchFindings({ subagentType: agentType, prompt: "", transport: "workflow" }))) continue;
+    const window = script.slice(agentTypeRe.lastIndex, Math.min(script.length, agentTypeRe.lastIndex + 4000));
+    const key = /\bprompt\s*(:\s*|(?=[,}]))/.exec(window);
+    if (!key) continue; // no prompt field nearby -> not a dispatch call
+    const open = /^([`'"])/.exec(window.slice(key.index + key[0].length));
+    if (!key[1] || !open) { unresolved.push({ agentType, cause: "its prompt is not a string literal (an identifier or expression)" }); continue; }
+    const start = key.index + key[0].length + 1;
+    let i = start;
+    let body = null;
+    while (i < window.length) {
+      if (window[i] === "\\") { i += 2; continue; }
+      if (window[i] === open[1]) { body = window.slice(start, i); break; }
+      i += 1;
+    }
+    if (body === null) unresolved.push({ agentType, cause: "its prompt literal does not terminate within the scanned window" });
+    else if (body.includes("${")) unresolved.push({ agentType, cause: "its prompt contains a ${...} interpolation" });
+  }
+  return unresolved;
+}
+
+export function workflowPromptUnresolvedNotice(unresolved) {
+  const causes = [...new Set(unresolved.map(({ cause }) => cause))].join("; ");
+  return `WARNING (guard-dispatch): GUARD-DISPATCH-WORKFLOW-PROMPT-UNRESOLVED: ${unresolved.length} Goldfish agent() call(s) in this Workflow script cannot be resolved statically (${causes}). The call is admitted, and no route record was written for this call, so the V-10 briefing-route binding does not apply to it. Repair: write the prompt as a static string literal (no \${...}, no variable) built from templates/prompts/goldfish-task.md, as plugins/pipeline-core/skills/pipeline-start/references/workflow-dispatch.md describes.\n`;
+}
+
 // Recover `{ TypeName: '...', Prompt: '...' }`-shaped entries from the Antigravity runner's
 // native `invoke_subagent` payload: `toolInput.Subagents` is an array, one entry per dispatched
 // subagent. An entry with no string `TypeName` carries nothing to check against a role template
@@ -704,6 +743,9 @@ if (isDirectInvocation(import.meta.url)) {

     dispatches = extractWorkflowDispatches(toolInput.script).map((dispatch) => ({ ...dispatch, transport: "workflow" }));
+    // ALF-K35: an unresolvable Goldfish prompt stays admitted (fail-open) but is announced, never silent.
+    const unresolvedWorkflow = findUnresolvedWorkflowGoldfishPrompts(toolInput.script);

-function extractRiskClass(specText) {
-  const m = /^\|\s*risk class\s*\|\s*([^|]+?)\s*\|/im.exec(specText);
-  if (!m) return null;
-  const word = /^[a-z]+/i.exec(m[1].trim());
-  return word ? word[0].toLowerCase() : null;
+const RISK_VOCABULARY = new Set(["low", "medium", "high"]);
+
+/** Markdown emphasis and code-span marks are presentation, not part of a cell's meaning. */
+function stripMarkup(cell) {
+  return String(cell ?? "").replace(/[*_`]/g, "").trim();
+}
+
+/**
+ * The first `| Risk class | <value> |` row of the spec's ID card.
+ *   { kind: "low" | "medium" | "high" }  a classified feature (templates/spec.md vocabulary)
+ *   { kind: "absent" }                   no row, or a value with no leading word (an unfilled
+ *                                        template placeholder): the author never classified it
+ *   { kind: "unrecognized", value }      a row whose leading word is outside the vocabulary
+ * Emphasis marks around the label or the value (`**Risk class**`, `**high**`, `high`) are
+ * ignored: a row that plainly says high must not read as "no row" because of its styling.
+ */
+function readRiskRow(specText) {
+  for (const line of String(specText).split(/\r?\n/)) {
+    const cells = /^\s*\|([^|]*)\|([^|]*)\|/.exec(line);
+    if (!cells || stripMarkup(cells[1]).toLowerCase() !== "risk class") continue;
+    const value = stripMarkup(cells[2]);
+    const word = /^[a-z]+/i.exec(value);
+    if (!word) return { kind: "absent" };
+    const lowered = word[0].toLowerCase();
+    return RISK_VOCABULARY.has(lowered)
+      ? { kind: lowered }
+      : { kind: "unrecognized", value: value.slice(0, 40) };
+  }
+  return { kind: "absent" };
 }

+#!/usr/bin/env node
+// SPDX-License-Identifier: SUL-1.0
+/**
+ * guard-git-gg22.test.mjs -- the GG-22 (backlog status-flip debt) cases, split out of guard-git.test.mjs
+ * (architecture boundary ratchet: split it rather than raise it). The cases below are moved verbatim and keep
+ * their ids (GG22-1 .. GG22-15, GG22-T51a-f, GG22-T5F1a-c, GG22-T5F4a-b). The fixture helpers (runGuard,
+ * check, gitIn, gitRepoFixture, commitFile) are minimal verbatim copies of the ones in guard-git.test.mjs,
+ * which does not export them.
+ *
+ * Run:   node plugins/pipeline-core/hooks/guard-git-gg22.test.mjs
+ * Exit:  0 = all cases pass · 1 = at least one case failed (failure list on stdout).
+ *
+ * Hermetics: every spawn sets CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR to a temp dir so a real project
+ * guard-config on the machine can never leak into union expectations.
+ */
+import { spawnSync } from "node:child_process";
+import { mkdtempSync, mkdirSync, realpathSync, writeFileSync, rmSync } from "node:fs";
+import { tmpdir } from "node:os";
+import { join } from "node:path";
+import { fileURLToPath } from "node:url";
+
+import { createGovernanceScopeController } from "../lib/governance-scope.mjs";
+
+const enrolledFixtureRoots = new Set();
+function enrollFixtureGovernance(root) {
+  if (enrolledFixtureRoots.has(root)) return;
+  const initialized = spawnSync("git", ["init", "-q", root], { encoding: "utf8" });
+  if (initialized.status !== 0) throw new Error(`fixture Git initialization failed: ${initialized.stderr}`);
+  const controller = createGovernanceScopeController({ hostStateRoot: join(root, ".git", "fixture-hoststate") });
+  const inactive = controller.observe({ rootDir: root });
+  if (inactive.state !== "inactive" || inactive.requiresEnforcement) throw new Error("fixture governance was not initially inactive");
+  const plan = controller.planDecision({ rootDir: root, decision: "enroll", by: "disposable-guard-fixture" });
+  const active = controller.applyDecision(plan, { activate: true, planSha256: plan.planSha256 });
+  if (active.state !== "active" || !active.requiresEnforcement) throw new Error("fixture enrollment did not activate enforcement");
+  enrolledFixtureRoots.add(root);
+}
+
+const GUARD = fileURLToPath(new URL("./guard-git.mjs", import.meta.url));
+
+/**
+ * Run the guard exactly like Claude Code does: tool-input JSON on stdin.
+ *
+ * Hermetics (P4-01): the base env is stripped of PIPELINE_GUARD_OVERRIDE before
+ * spreading, so a machine/session-level arming can never leak into a case that
+ * didn't ask for it. `envOverride` lets an override-mechanism case opt back into a
+ * session-level arming deliberately (AC-1 env-fallback path).
+ */
+function runGuard(command, projectDir, envOverride = {}) {
+  enrollFixtureGovernance(projectDir);
+  const { PIPELINE_GUARD_OVERRIDE: _dropInherited, ...baseEnv } = process.env;
+  const res = spawnSync(process.execPath, [GUARD], {
+    input: JSON.stringify({ tool_input: { command } }),
+    encoding: "utf8",
+    cwd: projectDir,
+    env: { ...baseEnv, CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR: projectDir, ...envOverride },
+  });
+  return { code: res.status, stderr: res.stderr ?? "" };
+}
+
+// Hermetic default project dir for tests unrelated to provenance. Those cases
+// opt out explicitly so the mandatory GIT-03 default cannot mask the rule each
+// case is intended to exercise.
+const EMPTY_DIR = mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), "guard-test-empty-"));
+mkdirSync(join(EMPTY_DIR, ".claude"), { recursive: true });
+writeFileSync(join(EMPTY_DIR, ".claude", "guard-config.json"), JSON.stringify({ commitTrailerPolicy: "off" }));
+
+let pass = 0;
+const failures = [];
+function check(id, command, expectExit, { projectDir = EMPTY_DIR, stderrIncludes, stderrExcludes, stderrEmpty, env = {} } = {}) {
+  const { code, stderr } = runGuard(command, projectDir, env);
+  const problems = [];
+  if (code !== expectExit) problems.push(`exit ${code} (expected ${expectExit})`);
+  for (const needle of [].concat(stderrIncludes ?? [])) {
+    if (!stderr.includes(needle)) problems.push(`stderr missing "${needle}"`);
+  }
+  for (const needle of [].concat(stderrExcludes ?? [])) {
+    if (stderr.includes(needle)) problems.push(`stderr unexpectedly contains "${needle}"`);
+  }
+  if (stderrEmpty && stderr.trim() !== "") problems.push(`stderr not empty: ${stderr.trim().slice(0, 120)}`);
+  if (problems.length === 0) {
+    pass++;
+    console.log(`PASS  ${id}`);
+  } else {
+    failures.push(`${id}: ${problems.join("; ")} — cmd: ${command}`);
+    console.log(`FAIL  ${id} — ${problems.join("; ")}`);
+  }
+}
+const BLOCK = 2, ALLOW = 0;
+
+// Fixture repos created by gitRepoFixture are removed in the summary below.
+const SIGNED_ROOTS = [];
+function gitIn(root) {
+  return (...args) => spawnSync(
+    "git",
+    ["-C", root, "-c", "user.email=guard-test@example.invalid", "-c", "user.name=Guard Test", "-c", "commit.gpgsign=false", ...args],
+    { encoding: "utf8" },
+  );
+}
+function gitRepoFixture(prefix) {
+  const root = realpathSync(mkdtempSync(join(tmpdir(), prefix)));
+  SIGNED_ROOTS.push(root);
+  gitIn(root)("init", "--quiet");
+  // Enroll before signed fixtures write legacy lifecycle/history records.
+  enrollFixtureGovernance(root);
+  return root;
+}
+function commitFile(root, name, body) {
+  writeFileSync(join(root, name), body);
+  gitIn(root)("add", "--", name);
+  gitIn(root)("commit", "--quiet", "-m", `seed ${name}`);
+}
+
+// ---- GG-22: a commit must not leave an earlier backlog status-flip unreconciled since -----
+// the last backlog/transitions.ndjson touch -------------------------------------------------
+//
+// Builds on the already-established real-git-repo primitives above (gitRepoFixture/gitIn/
+// commitFile, used unmodified for the SIG-* cases) rather than a second git-repo mechanism.
+// New, additive helpers used only by the GG22-* cases below -- the plain-temp-dir fixtures
+// elsewhere in this file (EMPTY_DIR and friends) are untouched.
+function gg22ItemBody(status, triage = "Demo triage text.") {
+  return `---\nid: demo-item\nstatus: ${status}\ntitle: demo backlog item\n---\n\nTriage: ${triage}\n`;
+}
+function gg22CommitItem(root, name, status, message, triage) {
+  mkdirSync(join(root, "backlog", "items"), { recursive: true });
+  writeFileSync(join(root, "backlog", "items", name), gg22ItemBody(status, triage));
+  gitIn(root)("add", "--", `backlog/items/${name}`);
+  gitIn(root)("commit", "--quiet", "-m", message);
+}
+function gg22CommitLedgerTouch(root, message = "chore: reconcile backlog ledger", seq = 1) {
+  mkdirSync(join(root, "backlog"), { recursive: true });
+  writeFileSync(join(root, "backlog", "transitions.ndjson"), `{"seq":${seq}}\n`);
+  gitIn(root)("add", "--", "backlog/transitions.ndjson");
+  gitIn(root)("commit", "--quiet", "-m", message);
+}
+function gg22StageFile(root, relPath, body) {
+  // These fixtures isolate GG-22. Keep the independent GIT-03 default from
+  // masking the rule and remediation text each case is designed to exercise.
+  mkdirSync(join(root, ".claude"), { recursive: true });
+  writeFileSync(join(root, ".claude", "guard-config.json"), JSON.stringify({ commitTrailerPolicy: "off" }));
+  const segments = relPath.split("/").slice(0, -1);
+  if (segments.length > 0) mkdirSync(join(root, ...segments), { recursive: true });
+  writeFileSync(join(root, relPath), body);
+  gitIn(root)("add", "--", relPath);
+}
+
+// GG22-1: bootstrap/no-op -- backlog/transitions.ndjson never existed in this repo's history.
+const GG22_BOOT_DIR = gitRepoFixture("guard-test-gg22-bootstrap-");
+commitFile(GG22_BOOT_DIR, "seed.txt", "seed\n");
+gg22StageFile(GG22_BOOT_DIR, "notes.txt", "hello\n");
+check(
+  "GG22-1 allow  bootstrap: backlog/transitions.ndjson never existed in history",
+  'git commit -m "chore: add notes"',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: GG22_BOOT_DIR },
+);
+
+// GG22-2: clean -- the status flip is reconciled by a LATER ledger touch, nothing outstanding.
+const GG22_CLEAN_DIR = gitRepoFixture("guard-test-gg22-clean-");
+commitFile(GG22_CLEAN_DIR, "seed.txt", "seed\n");
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_CLEAN_DIR, "demo.md", "open", "chore: add demo item (open)");
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_CLEAN_DIR, "demo.md", "in_progress", "chore: flip demo item to in_progress");
+gg22CommitLedgerTouch(GG22_CLEAN_DIR);
+gg22StageFile(GG22_CLEAN_DIR, "src/app.mjs", "export const x = 1;\n");
+check(
+  "GG22-2 allow  clean: status flip reconciled by a later ledger touch, nothing outstanding",
+  'git commit -m "feat: add app"',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: GG22_CLEAN_DIR },
+);
+
+// GG22-3: debt -- the status flip happened AFTER the last ledger touch, disallowed path staged.
+const GG22_DEBT_DIR = gitRepoFixture("guard-test-gg22-debt-");
+commitFile(GG22_DEBT_DIR, "seed.txt", "seed\n");
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_DEBT_DIR, "demo.md", "open", "chore: add demo item (open)");
+gg22CommitLedgerTouch(GG22_DEBT_DIR);
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_DEBT_DIR, "demo.md", "in_progress", "chore: flip demo item to in_progress");
+gg22StageFile(GG22_DEBT_DIR, "src/app.mjs", "export const x = 1;\n");
+check(
+  "GG22-3 block  debt: status flip after the last ledger touch, disallowed path staged",
+  'git commit -m "feat: add app"',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: GG22_DEBT_DIR, stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md", "reconcile-backlog-ledger.mjs --activate"] },
+);
+
+// GG22-4: the reconciliation commit itself -- only backlog/items/**+ledger paths staged, debt
+// exists but this IS the fixing commit and must not be blocked from happening.
+const GG22_FIX_DIR = gitRepoFixture("guard-test-gg22-fixcommit-");
+commitFile(GG22_FIX_DIR, "seed.txt", "seed\n");
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_FIX_DIR, "demo.md", "open", "chore: add demo item (open)");
+gg22CommitLedgerTouch(GG22_FIX_DIR);
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_FIX_DIR, "demo.md", "in_progress", "chore: flip demo item to in_progress");
+gg22StageFile(GG22_FIX_DIR, "backlog/transitions.ndjson", `{"seq":2}\n`);
+check(
+  "GG22-4 allow  reconciliation commit: staged commit touches only backlog/items+ledger paths",
+  'git commit -m "chore: reconcile backlog ledger"',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: GG22_FIX_DIR },
+);
+
+// GG22-5: item touched since reconcile, but its status: line did not change (Triage-only edit).
+const GG22_TRIAGE_DIR = gitRepoFixture("guard-test-gg22-triage-only-");
+commitFile(GG22_TRIAGE_DIR, "seed.txt", "seed\n");
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_TRIAGE_DIR, "demo.md", "open", "chore: add demo item (open)");
+gg22CommitLedgerTouch(GG22_TRIAGE_DIR);
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_TRIAGE_DIR, "demo.md", "open", "chore: update demo item triage prose", "Updated triage prose, no status change.");
+gg22StageFile(GG22_TRIAGE_DIR, "src/app.mjs", "export const x = 1;\n");
+check(
+  "GG22-5 allow  non-status edit: item touched since reconcile but its status: line did not change",
+  'git commit -m "feat: add app"',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: GG22_TRIAGE_DIR },
+);
+
+// GG22-6: fail-open -- a spawnSync git failure (PATH broken for the guard's own child process)
+// must never block, even against the exact same debt state that GG22-3 proves DOES block.
+check(
+  "GG22-6 allow  fail-open: a spawnSync git failure never blocks (same debt state as GG22-3)",
+  'git commit -m "feat: add app"',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: GG22_DEBT_DIR, env: { PATH: "/nonexistent-guard-test-bin" } },
+);
+
+// GG22-7: the corrected remediation order (item edits committed FIRST, reconciler run ONCE, ledger
+// commit LAST) drains debt cleanly -- this is the exact sequence that trapped an agent following the
+// OLD (reconcile-first) printed order live on 2026-08-29
+// (backlog/items/2026-08-29-gg-22s-own-remediation-order-creates-unclearable-ledger-debt.md). Batches
+// two item closures into one commit (the guard's own code comment: "batched multi-item closures ...
+// before one shared reconciliation commit are an established, legitimate pattern").
+const GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR = gitRepoFixture("guard-test-gg22-fixed-order-");
+commitFile(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "seed.txt", "seed\n");
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "one.md", "open", "chore: add item one (open)");
+gg22CommitItem(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "two.md", "open", "chore: add item two (open)");
+gg22CommitLedgerTouch(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "chore: reconcile backlog ledger", 1);
+// Batch-close both items in one commit -- item edits committed FIRST, per the corrected order.
+mkdirSync(join(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "backlog", "items"), { recursive: true });
+writeFileSync(join(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "backlog", "items", "one.md"), gg22ItemBody("closed"));
+writeFileSync(join(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "backlog", "items", "two.md"), gg22ItemBody("closed"));
+gitIn(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR)("add", "--", "backlog/items/one.md", "backlog/items/two.md");
+gitIn(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR)("commit", "--quiet", "-m", "chore: close items one and two");
+// Reconcile ONCE, ledger commit LAST -- after the item commit, not before it.
+gg22CommitLedgerTouch(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "chore: reconcile backlog ledger", 2);
+gg22StageFile(GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR, "src/app.mjs", "export const x = 1;\n");
+check(
+  "GG22-7 allow  fixed order: item edits committed first, reconciler run once, ledger commit last -- no debt",
+  'git commit -m "feat: add app"',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: GG22_FIXED_ORDER_DIR },
+);
+
+// GG22-8: the remediation guidance TEXT itself states the corrected order -- item edits first,
+// reconciler once, ledger commit last -- and reuses GG22-3's own debt fixture (same BLOCK case) to
+// prove the printed message no longer tells an agent to commit the ledger "before any other commit"
+// (the phrasing that forced the inverted, debt-trapping order) and instead names the item-edits-first,
+// ledger-last sequence.
+check(
+  "GG22-8 block  remediation text states the corrected reconcile-last order, not reconcile-first",
+  'git commit -m "feat: add app"',
+  BLOCK,
+  {
+    projectDir: GG22_DEBT_DIR,
+    stderrIncludes: [
+      "GG-22",
+      "Commit any pending backlog/items/ status edits first",
+      "then commit the resulting backlog/STATUS.md / backlog/index.json / backlog/transitions.ndjson changes last.",
+    ],
+  },
+);
+
+// GG22-9 through GG22-15: a pathspec'd commit is scoped to the paths it names only when
+// the invocation is provably pathspec-exclusive. Each fixture builds real history with status-flip
+// debt and a deliberately unrelated staged source file: a pass must come from the fixed commit
+// pathspec handling, never from an empty index or absent debt.
+function gg22PathspecDebtFixture(prefix) {
+  const root = gitRepoFixture(prefix);
+  commitFile(root, "seed.txt", "seed\n");
+  gg22CommitItem(root, "demo.md", "open", "chore: add demo item (open)");
+  gg22CommitLedgerTouch(root);
+  gg22CommitItem(root, "demo.md", "in_progress", "chore: flip demo item to in_progress");
+  gg22StageFile(root, "backlog/transitions.ndjson", "{\"seq\":2}\n");
+  gg22StageFile(root, "src/unrelated.mjs", "export const unrelated = true;\n");
+  return root;
+}
+
+const GG22_PATHSPEC_ALLOWED_DIR = gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-pathspec-allowed-");
+check(
+  "GG22-9 allow  pathspec ledger commit ignores unrelated staged source file",
+  'git commit -m "chore: reconcile ledger" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: GG22_PATHSPEC_ALLOWED_DIR },
+);
+
+const GG22_PATHSPEC_DISALLOWED_DIR = gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-pathspec-disallowed-");
+check(
+  "GG22-10 block  pathspec source commit remains blocked while GG22 debt exists",
+  'git commit -m "feat: source change" -- src/unrelated.mjs',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: GG22_PATHSPEC_DISALLOWED_DIR, stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+
+const GG22_PATHSPEC_BARE_DIR = gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-pathspec-bare-");
+check(
+  "GG22-11 block  bare staged-index commit remains blocked with unrelated source staged",
+  'git commit -m "feat: source change"',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: GG22_PATHSPEC_BARE_DIR, stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+
+const GG22_PATHSPEC_INCLUDE_SHORT_DIR = gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-pathspec-include-short-");
+check(
+  "GG22-12 block  -i widens a pathspec commit to the staged index",
+  'git commit -i -m "chore: reconcile ledger" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: GG22_PATHSPEC_INCLUDE_SHORT_DIR, stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+
+const GG22_PATHSPEC_INCLUDE_LONG_DIR = gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-pathspec-include-long-");
+check(
+  "GG22-13 block  --include widens a pathspec commit to the staged index",
+  'git commit --include -m "chore: reconcile ledger" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: GG22_PATHSPEC_INCLUDE_LONG_DIR, stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+
+const GG22_PATHSPEC_TRAVERSAL_DIR = gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-pathspec-traversal-");
+check(
+  "GG22-14 block  ../ traversal escaping backlog/items is disallowed",
+  'git commit -m "feat: source change" -- backlog/items/../../src/unrelated.mjs',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: GG22_PATHSPEC_TRAVERSAL_DIR, stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+
+const GG22_PATHSPEC_TRAILING_DIR = gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-pathspec-trailing-");
+gg22StageFile(GG22_PATHSPEC_TRAILING_DIR, "backlog/items/repair.md", gg22ItemBody("closed"));
+check(
+  "GG22-15 allow  trailing-slash backlog/items directory pathspec is admitted",
+  'git commit -m "chore: close item" -- backlog/items/',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: GG22_PATHSPEC_TRAILING_DIR },
+);
+
+// ---- TR-C: GG-22 pathspec fast path admits --trailer (toil-resolution row T51) -------------------
+// Added by the tranche-2 post-image of dispatch TR-C-T-20261009. Today --trailer (and its value token) is in
+// neither PATHSPEC_EXCLUSIVE_SAFE_FLAGS nor the value-flag lists, so a pathspec ledger commit that carries
+// trailers is not provably exclusive, falls through to git diff --cached and is blocked by an unrelated staged
+// source file (GG22-9 shows the same commit without trailers is allowed). T51a and T51b are RED on that account.
+// T51c to T51e pin that the addition widens nothing (I3): a source path stays blocked, -i stays widening, and
+// --no-verify stays a hook bypass (GG-17). -n and -c are deliberately not pinned: -n is already in the GG-22
+// safe list (a message/display flag there, unrelated to hook bypass) and which guard rule catches -n is unverified.
+const GG22_T51_LEDGER_TRAILERS = 'git commit -m "chore: reconcile ledger" --trailer "AI-Assisted: true" --trailer "Dispatch: TR-C-T-20261009 (goldfish)" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson';
+check(
+  "GG22-T51a allow  pathspec ledger commit carrying --trailer values ignores the unrelated staged source file",
+  GG22_T51_LEDGER_TRAILERS,
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t51a-") },
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T51b allow  the --trailer=<value> spelling is equally a safe flag on a pathspec ledger commit",
+  'git commit -m "chore: reconcile ledger" --trailer="AI-Assisted: true" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t51b-") },
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T51c block  --trailer does not let a source-path pathspec commit past GG-22",
+  'git commit -m "feat: source change" --trailer "AI-Assisted: true" -- src/unrelated.mjs',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t51c-"), stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T51d block  -i beside --trailer still widens a pathspec commit to the staged index",
+  'git commit -i -m "chore: reconcile ledger" --trailer "AI-Assisted: true" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t51d-"), stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T51e block  --no-verify beside --trailer stays blocked (I3, hook bypass; exit code only)",
+  'git commit --no-verify -m "chore: reconcile ledger" --trailer "AI-Assisted: true" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t51e-") },
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T51f text   --no-verify beside --trailer is still reported as GG-17 (RED until T51a/b land: today another rule answers first, exit 2 but no GG-17 text)",
+  'git commit --no-verify -m "chore: reconcile ledger" --trailer "AI-Assisted: true" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t51f-"), stderrIncludes: ["GG-17"] },
+);
+
+// ---- TR-C-T5: consumed-value flags before -i, and git --help (TR-C Critic findings F1 and F4) -----
+// Added by the tranche-2 post-image of dispatch TR-C-T5-20261009. F1: the GG-22 pathspec fast path skips a token
+// whenever the PREVIOUS token is a value-taking flag, even when that previous token was itself consumed as a value.
+// git reads the second --trailer in "--trailer --trailer -i" as the VALUE of the first, and the second -m in
+// "-m <subject> -m -m -i" likewise, so the following -i is a real --include that widens the commit to the staged index
+// (the unrelated staged source file of the fixture). T5F1a and T5F1b are RED while the skip stays unconditional.
+// T5F1c is a GREEN control: an ordinary second -m paragraph stays admitted. The subject is a conventional one on
+// purpose: GIT-01 answers first for a bare x subject, which would make the pin pass for the wrong rule.
+// NOT pinned: "-m <subject> -m -i -- <ledger>" (the briefed second spelling). There git takes -i as the VALUE of the
+// second -m, a message paragraph, so no --include exists; admitting it is correct and a block would be a false positive.
+// F4: git --help is pinned as TR-C-F-MANIFEST.md declares it: collapsed like --version, so alone it is a bare git word
+// and a following dangerous subcommand stays visible. Both are GREEN by design (the behaviour exists; it had no pin).
+check(
+  "GG22-T5F1a block  --trailer --trailer -i: the second --trailer is the first one's value, so -i is a real --include",
+  'git commit -m "chore: reconcile ledger" --trailer --trailer -i -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t5f1a-"), stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T5F1b block  -m <subject> -m -m -i: the third -m is the second one's value, so -i is a real --include",
+  'git commit -m "chore: reconcile ledger" -m -m -i -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  BLOCK,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t5f1b-"), stderrIncludes: ["GG-22", "backlog/items/demo.md"] },
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T5F1c allow  control: an ordinary second -m paragraph on a pathspec ledger commit stays admitted",
+  'git commit -m "chore: reconcile ledger" -m "second paragraph" -- backlog/transitions.ndjson',
+  ALLOW,
+  { projectDir: gg22PathspecDebtFixture("guard-test-gg22-t5f1c-") },
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T5F4a allow  git --help alone collapses to a bare git word and blocks nothing",
+  "git --help",
+  ALLOW,
+);
+check(
+  "GG22-T5F4b block  git --help collapses like --version, so a following reset --hard stays visible to the destructive rules",
+  "git --help reset --hard HEAD~1",
+  BLOCK,
+);
+
+// ---- Summary -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+for (const dir of [EMPTY_DIR, ...SIGNED_ROOTS]) {
+  try {
+    rmSync(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
+  } catch {
+    /* temp cleanup is best-effort */
+  }
+}
+const total = pass + failures.length;
+console.log(`\n${pass}/${total} cases passed.`);
+if (failures.length > 0) {
+  console.log("Failures:");
+  for (const f of failures) console.log(`  - ${f}`);
+  process.exit(1);
+}
+process.exit(0);

+/** The criterion shapes the host parser locates, as named in a refusal (D64: the repair is stated where the refusal is). */
+export const SUPPORTED_CRITERION_SHAPES = Object.freeze([
+  'a three-column table with the header `| # | Criterion | Evidence |` and the id (AC-<n>) in the first cell',
+  'a two-column table with the header `| # | Criterion |` (or `| Nr. | Kriterium |`) whose first cell is `n`, `R-n` or `AC-n`',
+  'a heading `AC-<n>` followed by one paragraph',
+  'a bullet `- **AC-<n>:** text` or a numbered item `1. **AC-<n>:** text`',
+]);
+export const CRITERION_ID_RULE = 'an id is AC-<n> (n without a leading zero) or AC-E<epic>-<story>-<nn>';
+const CRITERION_CANDIDATE_TOKEN = 'AC-[A-Za-z0-9]+(?:-[A-Za-z0-9]+)*';
+const TABLE_CANDIDATE = new RegExp(`^\\s*\\|\\s*(?:\\*\\*|\`)?(${CRITERION_CANDIDATE_TOKEN})(?:\\*\\*|\`)?\\s*\\|`, 'u');
+const ITEM_CANDIDATE = new RegExp(`^\\s*(?:[-+*]|[0-9]+[.)])\\s+(?:\\*\\*|\`)?(${CRITERION_CANDIDATE_TOKEN})(?:\\*\\*|\`)?[:.)]?(?:\\s|$|\\*)`, 'u');
+const HEADING_CANDIDATE = new RegExp(`^ {0,3}#{1,6}\\s+(${CRITERION_CANDIDATE_TOKEN})(?:\\s+#+)?\\s*$`, 'u');
+
+/**
+ * D64 (present-plan): is every acceptance-criterion row of the Spec one the registration and dispatch parser locates?
+ * Reuses `scanAcceptedCriterionRows` for what is located; the only addition is the list of CANDIDATE rows under a
+ * normative acceptance heading (a line that starts with an `AC-` token in a table cell, list item or heading), so a
+ * row the scan silently ignores -- the four-column table, a leading-zero id -- is named instead of first failing at
+ * dispatch preparation. A Spec with no candidate row is outside this check (the delivery hand-off, which also wants
+ * a row to exist, is a separate and deferred concern, PO decision D85). `source` is the strictly decoded Spec text.
+ * @returns {{ok: true, located: string[]} | {ok: false, code: 'CAC-CRITERION-UNSUPPORTED', located: string[], unsupported: {id: string, line: number, reason: string}[], message: string}}
+ */
+export function inspectSpecCriterionRows(source) {
+  const rows = scanAcceptedCriterionRows(source);
+  const counts = new Map();
+  for (const row of rows) if (row.normative) counts.set(row.id, (counts.get(row.id) ?? 0) + 1);
+  const located = [...new Set(rows.filter(row => row.normative && row.valid !== false).map(row => row.id))];
+  const unsupported = [];
+  for (const row of rows) {
+    if (!row.normative) continue;
+    if (row.valid === false) unsupported.push({id: row.id, line: 0, reason: 'shape'});
+    else if (counts.get(row.id) > 1 && !unsupported.some(entry => entry.id === row.id && entry.reason === 'duplicate')) unsupported.push({id: row.id, line: 0, reason: 'duplicate'});
+  }
+  const headings = []; let comment = false, fence = null;
+  const lines = source.split(/\r?\n/u);
+  for (let index = 0; index < lines.length; index++) {
+    const text = lines[index];
+    if (comment || text.includes('<!--')) { if (text.includes('<!--')) comment = true; if (text.includes('-->')) comment = false; continue; }
+    const marker = text.match(/^ {0,3}(`{3,}|~{3,})(.*)$/u);
+    if (fence) { if (marker && marker[1][0] === fence.char && marker[1].length >= fence.length && marker[2].trim() === '') fence = null; continue; }
+    if (marker) { fence = {char: marker[1][0], length: marker[1].length}; continue; }
+    if (/^(?: {0,3}>| {4}|\t)/u.test(text)) continue;
+    const heading = text.match(/^ {0,3}(#{1,6})\s+(.+?)(?:\s+#+)?\s*$/u);
+    if (heading) {
+      while (headings.length && headings.at(-1).level >= heading[1].length) headings.pop();
+      headings.push({level: heading[1].length, text: heading[2]});
+    }
+    if (!headings.some(entry => NORMATIVE_HEADING.test(entry.text)) || headings.some(entry => EXCLUDED_HEADING.test(entry.text))) continue;
+    const token = (heading ? text.match(HEADING_CANDIDATE) : text.match(TABLE_CANDIDATE) ?? text.match(ITEM_CANDIDATE))?.[1];
+    if (!token) continue;
+    if (!isCriterionId(token)) unsupported.push({id: token, line: index + 1, reason: 'id'});
+    else if (!located.includes(token)) unsupported.push({id: token, line: index + 1, reason: 'shape'});
+  }
+  if (unsupported.length === 0) return {ok: true, located};
+  const why = {id: `not a criterion id (${CRITERION_ID_RULE}; a leading zero is not allowed)`, shape: 'row shape the parser does not locate', duplicate: 'stated more than once'};
+  const named = unsupported.slice(0, 8).map(entry => `${entry.id}${entry.line ? ` (line ${entry.line}` : ' ('}${entry.line ? ', ' : ''}${why[entry.reason]})`).join('; ');
+  return {ok: false, code: 'CAC-CRITERION-UNSUPPORTED', located, unsupported,
+    message: `acceptance criterion rows the registration and dispatch parser cannot locate: ${named}${unsupported.length > 8 ? '; ...' : ''}. Supported shapes: ${SUPPORTED_CRITERION_SHAPES.join('; ')}. ${CRITERION_ID_RULE}; a leading zero is not allowed. Re-render the row; the criterion text itself need not change.`};
+}
+

   let parserVersion = 'markdown-acceptance-table.v1';

-function fail(code) { return { ok: false, code }; }
+function fail(code) { return withDeviceLocalFinding({ ok: false, code }); }
+
+// Backlog item pipeline.approval-bound-design-package-lives-in-an-ignored-directory (device switch). The
+// bound set of a design approval has two kinds of members. The committed ones (package, Advisor receipt,
+// readiness file, PRD, Spec, the approval record) arrive with a clone. The host-observed readiness receipt
+// and the Advisor course live in private state (`.git/agent-pipeline/`) and never do. An approved plan does
+// not need them again (PO decision BL; the stored-approval reader never consults them), but the
+// pre-approval readers do, and on a second device they used to refuse with a bare code. These codes
+// mean "this device cannot find or re-derive the device-local part"; they are NOT tamper codes, which
+// (MISMATCH, DRIFT, digest codes) deliberately get no finding. The finding is additive: `code` and `ok` never change.
+const DEVICE_LOCAL_CODES = Object.freeze({
+  "DWP-READINESS-HOST-RECEIPT-UNAVAILABLE": { part: "readiness-host-receipt", location: ".git/agent-pipeline/design-readiness/" },
+  "DWP-READINESS-HOST-REPOSITORY-MISMATCH": { part: "repository-identity", location: ".git/agent-pipeline/design-readiness/" },
+  "DWP2-REPOSITORY-IDENTITY": { part: "repository-identity", location: ".git/agent-pipeline/design-advisor-courses/" },
+  "DWP2-DURABLE-COURSE": { part: "advisor-course", location: ".git/agent-pipeline/design-advisor-courses/" },
+  "DWP2-DURABLE-FAILURE": { part: "advisor-course", location: ".git/agent-pipeline/design-advisor-courses/" },
+});
+export const DEVICE_LOCAL_BOUND_SET_FINDING_SCHEMA = "pipeline.device-local-bound-set-finding.v1";
+
+/** The typed device-local finding for a refusal code, or null when the code is not a missing-device-local-part code. */
+export function deviceLocalFindingForCode(code) {
+  const entry = typeof code === "string" && Object.hasOwn(DEVICE_LOCAL_CODES, code) ? DEVICE_LOCAL_CODES[code] : null;
+  if (entry === null) return null;
+  const script = fileURLToPath(new URL("../scripts/pipeline-state.mjs", import.meta.url));
+  return {
+    schema: DEVICE_LOCAL_BOUND_SET_FINDING_SCHEMA,
+    part: entry.part,
+    location: entry.location,
+    explanation: `The ${entry.part} of this design is device-local private state (${entry.location}); it is not committed, so a checkout on another device does not have it. This is not tampering.`,
+    repair: {
+      argv: [script, "rebind-approval"],
+      text: `Run once from the project root: node ${script} rebind-approval. For an already approved plan it verifies the approval from the committed bound set alone (zero signatures, no State change); if no approval is recorded yet it answers DWP-REBIND-NO-APPROVAL and the design course must be run again on this device.`,
+    },
+  };
+}
+
+/** Adds `deviceLocal` to a refusal whose code names a missing device-local part; every other result is returned as is. */
+export function withDeviceLocalFinding(result) {
+  if (result === null || typeof result !== "object" || result.ok !== false || Object.hasOwn(result, "deviceLocal")) return result;
+  const finding = deviceLocalFindingForCode(result.code);
+  return finding === null ? result : { ...result, deviceLocal: finding };

 }
 
+/**
+ * PO decision D117 (decision BI, backlog item denial-names-a-diagnosis-it-refuses): an approved feature whose approval
+ * cannot be RE-VERIFIED degrades to an advisory instead of locking every write. Only a verification INPUT that is
+ * unavailable qualifies -- never a mismatch. Today that is exactly one admission code: `DWP-PACKAGE-PHYSICAL`, the approved
+ * design workflow package file is missing or is not a physical repository file, so its digest cannot be recomputed. Every
+ * code that says a record, digest, signature, candidate or source DIFFERS stays a block with its own code (the package
+ * digest drift is `DWP-APPROVAL-DIGEST-DRIFT`, a wrong signer is `DAA-PO-APPROVAL-TRUST-MISMATCH`, ...), and so does a
+ * missing trust-anchor setup (`DAA-PO-APPROVAL-TRUST-ANCHOR-SETUP`: nothing was ever configured to verify against).
+ * `designAdvisoryAdmission()` itself is NOT changed: its other callers (architecture-design, design-decision-bindings)
+ * need real authority and keep reading `ok:false`; only the two gate call sites consult this function.
+ */
+export const UNVERIFIABLE_APPROVAL_CODES = Object.freeze(["DWP-PACKAGE-PHYSICAL"]);
+export const UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE = "GUARD-DEVPLAN-APPROVAL-UNVERIFIED";
+
+/** @returns {null|{code:string,cause:string,text:string}} null unless the refusal is an unavailable verification input. */
+export function unverifiedApprovalAdvisory(admission, state) {
+  if (admission?.ok !== false || !UNVERIFIABLE_APPROVAL_CODES.includes(admission.code)) return null;
+  const packagePath = state?.planApproval?.designWorkflowPackagePath;
+  const where = typeof packagePath === "string" && packagePath !== "" ? ` at ${packagePath}` : "";
+  const text = [
+    `[guard-devplan] ADVISORY (${UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE}, cause ${admission.code}): the recorded design approval could not be re-verified, so this write was allowed on the recorded approval alone.`,
+    `What could not be verified: the approved design workflow package${where} is missing or is not a physical repository file, so its digest, signature and bound sources were NOT checked. Nothing was verified.`,
+    "Repair: run reopen-design --by <human attribution>, then finish the sources and obtain the approval again (a package that is present but different still blocks as DWP-APPROVAL-DIGEST-DRIFT).",
+  ].join("\n");
+  return { code: UNVERIFIED_APPROVAL_ADVISORY_CODE, cause: admission.code, text };
+}
+

 export const DISPATCH_RECORD_INTERIM_OUTCOME = "committed-pending-report";
@@ -181,6 +181,22 @@ export function claimDispatchRecordOwnership({ relPath, requested, root, input,
   }

+// PO decision 2026-10-22 (ALF-K34, "align the unclaimed path"): an UNCLAIMED record now admits what the claimed path already admits for a multi-commit
+// package -- the next post-commit checkpoint. The validator forces candidateCommit to the last commit, so the second checkpoint changes candidateCommit
+// against an interim record that the denylist calls terminal. Admitted only when the existing record is the INTERIM outcome, the incoming write is JSON
+// for the same task id that is still the interim outcome (not final, not in-progress), and its commits strictly EXTEND the existing list (prefix and
+// order preserved, nothing removed). Everything else keeps the previous refusal: a final record, another task id, a shrunk/reordered/replaced list,
+// an Edit-shaped (non-JSON) payload.
+function dispatchRecordInterimExtension({ taskId, existing, incoming }) {
+  const interim = (outcome) => String(outcome).trim().toLowerCase() === DISPATCH_RECORD_INTERIM_OUTCOME;
+  if (incoming === null || typeof incoming !== "object" || Array.isArray(incoming)) return false;
+  if (!interim(existing.outcome) || !interim(incoming.outcome)) return false;
+  if (incoming.taskId !== taskId || existing.taskId !== taskId) return false;
+  if (!Array.isArray(existing.commits) || !Array.isArray(incoming.commits)) return false;
+  if (incoming.commits.length <= existing.commits.length) return false;
+  return existing.commits.every((sha, index) => incoming.commits[index] === sha);
+}

+// D114 (PO decision 2026-10-22): `git -C <repo> show|log|diff|ls-files|cat-file ... | head -n N`, READ forms only. The -C target is
+// the SAME real, non-symlink directory inside the project/approved roots the un-piped `git -C` already requires, additionally a
+// repository top level (it has a `.git` entry, so `<rev>:<path>` and pathspecs mean the same directory the screen judges) and
+// cleared by the passive-read credential screen as a directory. Every operand is then judged against THAT directory with the same
+// functions the plain pipeline uses, and may not leave it. Nothing else changes: a second -C, a flag-shaped, glob, expansion or
+// escaping target, and every non-read subcommand are refused.
+const GIT_C_PIPELINE_SUBCOMMANDS = new Set(["show", "log", "diff", "ls-files", "cat-file"]);
+const GIT_CAT_FILE_MODES = new Set(["-p", "-t", "-s", "-e"]);
+const GIT_LS_FILES_FLAGS = new Set(["-c", "--cached", "-d", "--deleted", "-m", "--modified", "-s", "--stage", "-u", "--unmerged",
+  "-z", "--full-name", "--error-unmatch", "--exclude-standard", "--deduplicate", "--eol"]);
+
+function resolveGitCPipelineTarget(target, root, extraRoots) {
+  if (typeof target !== "string" || target === "" || target.startsWith("-") || /[\0$`*?\[\]{}~\\]/u.test(target)) return null;
+  try {
+    const directory = resolve(root, target);
+    const facts = lstatSync(directory);
+    if (!facts.isDirectory() || facts.isSymbolicLink()) return null;
+    const physical = realpathSync(directory);
+    if (!isApprovedSingleCommandReadArg(physical, root, extraRoots, true)) return null;
+    if (!isAllowedPassiveReadTarget(physical, { rootDir: root, directoryListing: true, additionalRecursiveRoots: extraRoots })) return null;
+    lstatSync(join(physical, ".git"));
+    return physical;
+  } catch { return null; }
+}
+
+function areGitLsFilesOperandsInside(subargs, gitDir) {
+  let afterDashDash = false;
+  for (const arg of subargs) {
+    if (!afterDashDash && arg === "--") { afterDashDash = true; continue; }
+    if (!afterDashDash && arg.startsWith("-")) {
+      if (!GIT_LS_FILES_FLAGS.has(arg)) return false;
+      continue;
+    }
+    if (!isGitPathOperandInsideRoot(arg, gitDir)) return false;
+  }
+  return true;
+}
+
+function isGitCPipelineOperandsAdmitted(subcommand, subargs, gitDir) {
+  if (!GIT_C_PIPELINE_SUBCOMMANDS.has(subcommand)) return false;
+  if (subcommand === "cat-file") {
+    // Closed form: exactly one of -p/-t/-s/-e and one object; no --batch*, --textconv, --filters or --follow-symlinks.
+    return subargs.length === 2 && GIT_CAT_FILE_MODES.has(subargs[0])
+      && (isStrictGitRevision(subargs[1]) || isContainedGitObjectSpec(subargs[1], gitDir));
+  }
+  if (!isReadOnlyGitSubcommand(subcommand, subargs)) return false;
+  if (!areGitObjectSpecOperandsContained(subcommand, subargs, gitDir)) return false;
+  if (!areGitPipelineOperandsContained(subargs, gitDir, [])) return false;
+  return subcommand === "ls-files"
+    ? areGitLsFilesOperandsInside(subargs, gitDir)
+    : areGitReadOperandsInsideRoot(subcommand, subargs, gitDir);
+}

+/**
+ * Validate a host-supplied family catalogue: the `records` of a normalized `pipeline.model-family-discovery.v1`
+ * (the repo's existing family declaration), with the same exact shapes and invariants. It proves membership and
+ * order only; it grants nothing. Returns null when the shape is not exactly that.
+ */
+function normalizeFamilyCatalogue(catalogue) {
+  if (!Array.isArray(catalogue) || catalogue.length === 0 || catalogue.length > 2048) return null;
+  const releaseIds = new Set();
+  const modelIds = new Set();
+  const versionsByFamily = new Map();
+  let variantCount = 0;
+  for (const record of catalogue) {
+    if (!hasKeys(record, RELEASE_KEYS) || !id(record.releaseId) || releaseIds.has(record.releaseId)
+      || !validFamilyId(record.familyId) || !validVersion(record.version) || typeof record.released !== "boolean"
+      || !SHA.test(record.groupingEvidenceSha256 ?? "")
+      || !Array.isArray(record.variants) || record.variants.length < 1 || record.variants.length > 256) return null;
+    releaseIds.add(record.releaseId);
+    const seen = versionsByFamily.get(record.familyId) ?? new Set();
+    if (seen.has(versionKey(record.version))) return null; // two releases of one family at one version: ambiguous order
+    seen.add(versionKey(record.version));
+    versionsByFamily.set(record.familyId, seen);
+    for (const variant of record.variants) {
+      variantCount += 1;
+      if (variantCount > 8192 || !hasKeys(variant, VARIANT_KEYS) || !id(variant.modelId) || modelIds.has(variant.modelId)
+        || !(variant.canonicalModelId === null || id(variant.canonicalModelId))
+        || !(variant.aliasEvidenceSha256 === null || SHA.test(variant.aliasEvidenceSha256 ?? ""))
+        || (variant.canonicalModelId === null) !== (variant.aliasEvidenceSha256 === null)
+        || typeof variant.visible !== "boolean" || typeof variant.selectable !== "boolean"
+        || (variant.selectable && (!variant.visible || !record.released))
+        || !(variant.efforts === null || (Array.isArray(variant.efforts) && variant.efforts.length <= 128
+          && variant.efforts.every((effort) => typeof effort === "string" && CATALOGUE_EFFORT.test(effort))
+          && new Set(variant.efforts).size === variant.efforts.length))
+        || !(variant.capabilityEvidenceSha256 === null || SHA.test(variant.capabilityEvidenceSha256 ?? ""))
+        || (variant.efforts !== null && variant.capabilityEvidenceSha256 === null)
+        || variant.compatibilityBySlotSha256 === null || typeof variant.compatibilityBySlotSha256 !== "object"
+        || Array.isArray(variant.compatibilityBySlotSha256)
+        || Object.keys(variant.compatibilityBySlotSha256).length > 512
+        || Object.entries(variant.compatibilityBySlotSha256).some(([key, value]) => key.length > 512
+          || !(value === null || SHA.test(value ?? "")))) return null;
+      modelIds.add(variant.modelId);
+    }
+  }
+  return catalogue;
+}
+
+/**
+ * D99: a newer VERSION inside the family the PO approved is followed without a new confirmation. Membership and
+ * order come only from the declared family (signed policy entry) and the host catalogue; an id that is not a
+ * released, visible, selectable, offered, effort-capable member of that family is never followed. A different
+ * family is never touched. Returns the followed variant (or null) and the ids the family rule has accounted for.
+ */
+function followApprovedFamily({ selected, effort, available, approvedIds, records }) {
+  let anchor = null;
+  for (const record of records) {
+    const variant = record.variants.find((item) => item.modelId === selected.modelId);
+    if (variant) anchor = record;
+  }
+  if (anchor === null) return { ok: true, follow: null, accounted: new Set() };
+  if (anchor.familyId !== selected.familyId || compareModelFamilyVersions(anchor.version, selected.version) !== 0) {
+    return { ok: false, code: "MODEL-ROLE-FAMILY-DECLARATION-MISMATCH" };
+  }
+  const members = records.filter((record) => record.familyId === selected.familyId && record.released);
+  const admissible = (record) => record.variants.filter((variant) => variant.visible && variant.selectable);
+  const newer = members.filter((record) => compareModelFamilyVersions(record.version, selected.version) > 0);
+  const followable = newer.map((record) => ({ record, variants: admissible(record).filter((variant) =>
+    available.has(variant.modelId) && !approvedIds.has(variant.modelId)
+    && Array.isArray(variant.efforts) && variant.efforts.includes(effort)) })).filter((item) => item.variants.length > 0);
+  let follow = null;
+  if (followable.length > 0) {
+    const best = followable.reduce((top, item) => compareModelFamilyVersions(item.record.version, top.record.version) > 0 ? item : top);
+    if (best.variants.length === 1) follow = { modelId: best.variants[0].modelId, version: [...best.record.version] };
+  }
+  const reached = follow === null ? selected.version : follow.version;
+  const accounted = new Set(members.filter((record) => compareModelFamilyVersions(record.version, reached) <= 0)
+    .flatMap((record) => admissible(record).map((variant) => variant.modelId)));
+  return { ok: true, follow, accounted };
 }

+// SPDX-License-Identifier: SUL-1.0
+/**
+ * Wire shape of the onboarding driver result printed by scripts/onboarding-init.mjs
+ * (pipeline.onboarding-init.v1). The library result (`driveOnboardingInit`) keeps every
+ * published action in each place a caller may read it; the printed form states each one once:
+ *
+ * - `pending-asks` and `collect-input` outcomes: the top-level `pendingAsks` is the single
+ *   copy; `final.nextAction.pendingAsks` and `collectInput.pendingAsks` are dropped.
+ * - `collect-input` outcome: `collectInput` is the single full copy of the primary action;
+ *   `final.nextAction` becomes `{ kind, see: "collectInput" }`.
+ *
+ * A copy is dropped only when it is canonically equal to the copy that is kept, so a divergent
+ * copy is never hidden or replaced. `resolvePrintedOnboardingInit()` is the inverse for a
+ * consumer that needs the library shape: it resolves the reference and restores the asks.
+ */
+
+function canonical(value) {
+  if (Array.isArray(value)) return `[${value.map(canonical).join(",")}]`;
+  if (value !== null && typeof value === "object") {
+    return `{${Object.keys(value).sort().map((key) => `${JSON.stringify(key)}:${canonical(value[key])}`).join(",")}}`;
+  }
+  return JSON.stringify(value);
+}
+
+const PRINTED_ONCE_OUTCOMES = ["pending-asks", "collect-input"];
+const withoutAsks = (action) => Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(action).filter(([key]) => key !== "pendingAsks"));
+
+export function printOnboardingInitResult(result) {
+  if (!PRINTED_ONCE_OUTCOMES.includes(result?.outcome)) return result;
+  const asks = result.pendingAsks;
+  const dropAsks = (action) => (Array.isArray(asks) && asks.length > 0 && Array.isArray(action?.pendingAsks)
+    && canonical(action.pendingAsks) === canonical(asks) ? withoutAsks(action) : action);
+  let nextAction = result.final?.nextAction ? dropAsks(result.final.nextAction) : undefined;
+  const collectInput = result.collectInput ? dropAsks(result.collectInput) : undefined;
+  if (result.outcome === "collect-input" && nextAction && collectInput && canonical(nextAction) === canonical(collectInput)) {
+    nextAction = { kind: collectInput.kind, see: "collectInput" };
+  }
+  return { ...result,
+    ...(collectInput ? { collectInput } : {}),
+    ...(nextAction ? { final: { ...result.final, nextAction } } : {}) };
+}
+
+export function resolvePrintedOnboardingInit(printed) {
+  if (!PRINTED_ONCE_OUTCOMES.includes(printed?.outcome)) return printed;
+  const asks = Array.isArray(printed.pendingAsks) && printed.pendingAsks.length > 0 ? printed.pendingAsks : null;
+  const withAsks = (action) => (action && asks && action.pendingAsks === undefined ? { ...action, pendingAsks: asks } : action);
+  const collectInput = withAsks(printed.collectInput);
+  const reference = printed.final?.nextAction?.see === "collectInput" && collectInput;
+  const nextAction = reference ? collectInput : withAsks(printed.final?.nextAction);
+  if (collectInput === printed.collectInput && nextAction === printed.final?.nextAction) return printed;
+  return { ...printed,
+    ...(collectInput ? { collectInput } : {}),
+    ...(nextAction ? { final: { ...printed.final, nextAction } } : {}) };
+}

-function userVisibleHostPath(path, homeDir) {
+// PO decision D109 (2026-10-22): name-based credential locations that do not depend on which platform runs the guard.
+// A path is classified by SHAPE, as a string: a home (the current one, another user's /home/<u> or /Users/<u>), or a
+// Windows drive (native C:\ or WSL /mnt/<letter>/), inside which C:\Users\<u> is a profile. The home-relative entries,
+// the whole AppData\Roaming and AppData\Local, the drive-level system folders and the reviewed named exceptions all come
+// from PASSIVE_READ_CREDENTIAL_ROOTS; this block holds no list of its own. Windows spellings compare case-insensitively.
+const fold = (values) => Object.freeze(values.map((value) => value.toLowerCase()));
+const HOME_ENTRIES_FOLDED = fold(PASSIVE_READ_CREDENTIAL_ROOTS.homeRelativePaths);
+const PROFILE_ENTRIES_FOLDED = fold(PASSIVE_READ_CREDENTIAL_ROOTS.windowsProfileRelativePaths);
+const WINDOWS_SYSTEM_FOLDED = fold(PASSIVE_READ_CREDENTIAL_ROOTS.windowsSystemRoots);
+const NATIVE_DRIVE = /^[a-z]:(?:[\\/](.*))?$/iu;
+const WSL_DRIVE = /^\/mnt(?:\/([a-z])(?:\/(.*))?)?$/u;
+const WINDOWS_PROFILE = /^users\/([^/]+)(?:\/(.*))?$/iu;
+const OTHER_USER_HOME = /^\/(?:home|Users)\/([^/]+)(?:\/(.*))?$/u;
+
+function classifyHostPath(identity, homePrefixes) {
+  let rest = null;
+  const native = NATIVE_DRIVE.exec(identity);
+  if (native !== null) rest = (native[1] ?? "").replaceAll("\\", "/");
+  else {
+    const wsl = WSL_DRIVE.exec(identity);
+    if (wsl !== null) rest = wsl[1] === undefined ? "" : (wsl[2] ?? "");
+  }
+  if (rest !== null) {
+    const profile = WINDOWS_PROFILE.exec(rest);
+    return profile === null ? { windows: true, drive: true, profile: false, rest }
+      : { windows: true, drive: true, profile: true, rest: profile[2] ?? "" };
+  }
+  for (const prefix of homePrefixes) {
+    if (identity === prefix) return { windows: false, drive: false, profile: false, rest: "" };
+    if (identity.startsWith(`${prefix}/`)) return { windows: false, drive: false, profile: false, rest: identity.slice(prefix.length + 1) };
+  }
+  const other = OTHER_USER_HOME.exec(identity);
+  return other === null ? null : { windows: false, drive: false, profile: false, rest: other[2] ?? "" };
+}
+
+function homeRestRefused(rest, windows, recursive, directoryListing) {
+  const probe = windows ? rest.toLowerCase() : rest;
+  if (probe === "") return recursive || directoryListing;
+  const entries = windows ? [...HOME_ENTRIES_FOLDED, ...PROFILE_ENTRIES_FOLDED] : PASSIVE_READ_CREDENTIAL_ROOTS.homeRelativePaths;
+  return entries.some((entry) => probe === entry || probe.startsWith(`${entry}/`)
+    || (recursive && entry.startsWith(`${probe}/`)));
+}
+
+function hostCredentialRefusal(info, recursive, directoryListing) {
+  if (info.drive && !info.profile) {
+    if (info.rest === "") return recursive;
+    const segments = info.rest.toLowerCase().split("/");
+    if (WINDOWS_SYSTEM_FOLDED.includes(segments[0])) return true;
+    return segments[0] === "users" && segments.length === 1 && recursive;
+  }
+  return homeRestRefused(info.rest, info.windows, recursive, directoryListing);
+}
+
+function hostException(info, now) {
+  if (info === null || (info.drive && !info.profile)) return null;
+  const rest = info.windows ? info.rest.toLowerCase() : info.rest;
+  return PASSIVE_READ_CREDENTIAL_ROOTS.namedExceptions.find((entry) => {
+    if ((entry.windowsOnly && !info.windows) || Date.parse(`${entry.expires}T23:59:59.999Z`) < now) return false;
+    const path = info.windows ? entry.path.toLowerCase() : entry.path;
+    if (entry.kind === "file") return rest === path;
+    if (entry.kind === "dir") return rest === path || rest.startsWith(`${path}/`);
+    if (entry.kind === "files-with-suffix") {
+      const suffix = info.windows ? entry.suffix.toLowerCase() : entry.suffix;
+      const name = rest.slice(path.length + 1);
+      return rest.startsWith(`${path}/`) && !name.includes("/") && name.endsWith(suffix) && name.length > suffix.length;
+    }
+    return false;
+  }) ?? null;
+}
+
+/** True when `identity` (an absolute path, either spelling) lies in a name-based credential location and no live named exception covers it. */
+export function isHostCredentialLocation(identity, { homePrefixes = [], recursive = false, directoryListing = false, now = Date.now() } = {}) {
+  if (typeof identity !== "string" || identity === "") return false;
+  const info = classifyHostPath(identity, homePrefixes);
+  return info !== null && hostException(info, now) === null && hostCredentialRefusal(info, recursive, directoryListing);
+}
+
+// PO decision D96 (2026-10-22, with the 2026-09-10 correction in docs/adr/draft-read-scope-containment-boundary.md):
+// a passive read outside the repository is not by itself a denial reason. Every absolute host path qualifies here;
+// the credential roots, system pseudo roots and secret basenames are screened for BOTH identities by the caller
+// before this predicate is reached.
+function userVisibleHostPath(path) {
   if (process.platform === "win32") return /^[a-z]:[\\/]/iu.test(path) && !systemPseudoPath(path);
-  return [homeDir, "/home", "/mnt", "/media", "/tmp", "/private/tmp",
-    "/Users", "/Volumes", "/workspace", "/workspaces", "/var/folders", "/private/var/folders"]
-    .some((root) => within(root, path));
+  return isAbsolute(path);
 }

+  ];

+  // The physical name counts too: an ordinary-looking outside link that resolves into an enrolled location.
+  for (const [link, target] of [["/opt/link/token", "/var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/serviceaccount/token"], ["/opt/link/tab", "/var/spool/cron/crontabs/alice"]]) {
+    assert.equal(isAllowedPassiveReadTarget(link, { ...context, realpath: (path) => (path === link ? target : path) }), false, `${link} -> ${target}`);
+  }
+  // A recursive search that would sweep an enrolled location is refused as well (its ancestors included).
+  for (const ancestor of ["/var/spool", "/var/run", "/var"]) {
+    assert.equal(isAllowedPassiveReadTarget(ancestor, { ...context, stat: () => directory, recursive: true }), false, `recursive ${ancestor}`);
+  }
+  // Positive controls: segment-exact matching, a public key, and plain outside paths are still admitted (D96).
+  for (const path of ["/var/spool/cronfoo/x", "/var/spool/mqueue/x", "/var/run/secretsfoo/x", "/opt/host-keys/ssh_host_rsa_key.pub",
+    "/usr/share/doc/readme", "/srv/data/report.md", "/opt/etc/ssl/private-notes.md"]) {
+    assert.equal(isAllowedPassiveReadTarget(path, context), true, `${path} must stay admitted`);
+  }
+  const real = join(fixture, "outside.txt");
+  writeFileSync(real, "plain\n");
+  assert.equal(isAllowedPassiveReadTarget(real, { rootDir: root, homeDir: home, machinePlaneRead: () => ({ status: "absent" }),
+    repoKeyDirectory: () => ({ status: "absent" }) }), true, "a real plain outside file is admitted without any injection");
+});
+
+// PO decision D109 (2026-10-22): the credential declaration follows external best practice on Linux, macOS, Windows and
+// WSL (catalogue: scratch/agent-out/sensitive-paths-research.md, sources S1-S52), with an explicit named-exceptions table.
+// Every case drives the real decision through the module's injection seams (identity realpath, plain-file stat, homeDir,
+// now): no host file is needed, the target need not exist, and nothing here claims to have run on Windows or macOS.
+const d109 = (homeDir = "/home/alice", extra = {}) => {
+  const plain = { isFile: () => true, isDirectory: () => false, nlink: 1 };
+  const dir = { isFile: () => false, isDirectory: () => true, nlink: 2 };
+  const context = {
+    rootDir: "/srv/d109-project", homeDir, machinePlaneRead: () => ({ status: "absent" }), repoKeyDirectory: () => ({ status: "absent" }),
+    realpath: (path) => extra.links?.[path] ?? path, stat: () => plain, ...extra,
+  };
+  return {
+    admits: (path, more = {}) => isAllowedPassiveReadTarget(path, { ...context, ...more }),
+    admitsDir: (path, more = {}) => isAllowedPassiveReadTarget(path, { ...context, stat: () => dir, ...more }),
+  };
+};
+
+test("D109 Linux home credential locations are refused (absolute and ~), with ordinary neighbours still admitted", (t) => {
+  if (process.platform === "win32") { t.skip("POSIX host paths"); return; }
+  const { admits } = d109();
+  const refused = [".config/google-chrome/Default/Login Data", ".config/chromium/Default/Cookies", ".mozilla/firefox/x.default/places.sqlite",
+    ".local/share/keyrings/login.keyring", ".local/share/kwalletd/kdewallet.kwl", ".password-store/site.gpg", ".gcm/store/x",
+    ".vault-token", ".config/git/credentials", ".terraformrc", ".terraform.d/credentials.tfrc.json", ".cargo/credentials.toml",
+    ".cargo/credentials", ".pgpass", ".cache/huggingface/token", ".gem/credentials", ".local/share/gem/credentials",
+    ".config/Bitwarden/data.json", ".config/Bitwarden CLI/data.json", ".config/op/config", ".op/config", ".config/pip/pip.conf",
+    ".bash_history", ".zsh_history", ".config/BraveSoftware/Brave-Browser/Default/Login Data"];
+  for (const entry of refused) {
+    assert.equal(admits(`/home/alice/${entry}`), false, `${entry} must be refused`);
+    assert.equal(admits(`~/${entry}`), false, `~/${entry} must be refused`);
+  }
+  for (const entry of [".cargo/config.toml", ".cache/huggingface/hub/models/x.txt", ".config/chromium-notes/x.md", ".terraform.d/plugins/x.txt",
+    ".config/git/config", "notes/passwords-policy.md", ".local/share/applications/x.desktop"]) {
+    assert.equal(admits(`/home/alice/${entry}`), true, `${entry} must stay admitted`);
+    assert.equal(admits(`~/${entry}`), true, `~/${entry} must stay admitted`);
+  }
+});
